===== ARCHITECTURE SUMMARY =====
 There are TWO parallel task substrates in laravel_main, and the maps conflate them: (A) the generic GlobalTask system (table global_tasks, model app/Models/GlobalTask.php, orchestrated by app/Services/TaskManagerService.php) with a 7-status lifecycle, an integer `priority` field, and mutually-exclusive `execution_type` lanes (remote_translation, remote_client, remote_audio, remote_notebooklm, remote_gemini, remote_compute, remote_ocr, remote_video, remote_io, local_timer); and (B) the AppQyV1 media queues that live as columns ON canonical dictionary rows (tts_status/tts_priority on AppQyV1LangDictionaryModel, image_status/image_priority), driven by the queue-less coordinators (AppQyV1UnifiedTTSQueueService, AppQyV1WordImageQueueService, AppQyV1DictionaryTTSCoordinator) plus an Octane timer, and a third assist_requests table surfaced by the wordflow UI. Workers connect over a no-auth HTTP worker API (POST /api/worker/register, GET /api/worker/tasks/pull long-poll, POST /api/worker/tasks/result, POST /api/worker/heartbeat). Pull is an atomic claim ordered by priority DESC, created_at ASC, scoped to the worker's processor_types — so each GlobalTask is claimed by exactly ONE worker lane. pycore (pycore/callmodule/services/translation_worker_service.py + tts_sentence_worker_service.py) registers for remote_translation/remote_audio and also consumes AppQyV1 sentence/word-audio claim endpoints; chrome-mcp (apps/mcp-chrome SimpleWorkerBase + WorkerApiClient) registers for remote_client/remote_notebooklm/remote_gemini. On completion, TaskProcessorRegistry routes results to processors (WordTranslationTaskProcessor, NotebookLmTaskProcessor, WordGeminiImageTaskProcessor) which write canonical data via AppQyV1WordTranslationWriteback. [2026-06-22 15:46:19] ASSISTANT:
The mapping is done and it's revealing. Here's the essential picture before I ask you to decide a few things.

## What's actually there today

There are **two parallel task systems** in `laravel_main`, and that split is the root of the "not enough / doesn't fully work" feeling:

- **Substrate A — the generic `GlobalTask` system** (`global_tasks` table, `TaskManagerService`, a real `priority` integer, mutually-exclusive `execution_type` lanes). Workers pull over a no-auth HTTP API (`/api/worker/tasks/pull` long-poll → `/result`). **Image** generation flows through here.
- **Substrate B — AppQyV1 media queues** that live as `tts_status`/`tts_priority`/`image_status` columns *on the dictionary rows themselves*, drained by Octane timers. **Translation** and **word audio** mostly flow through here; **sentence audio** is generated *synchronously inline*. There's also a third `assist_requests` table that the wordflow UI surfaces.

So a single "word/sentence" request today fans across three different mechanisms with three different priority columns — which is why one "jump to top" can't currently prioritize a word across translate+audio+image at once.

**Priority today:** a numeric field; "front of queue" = bump pending rows to `priority=100`; a `pending_urgent` count rides along in pull/heartbeat responses. **Gaps:** bumping only helps tasks still `pending` (no pre-emption of in-flight work); only the pycore *sentence* worker has a real priority heap (translation worker + chrome are FIFO and ignore `pending_urgent`); `execution_type` lanes are mutually exclusive so **one task = one client lane** (no built-in "both pycore and chrome process it"); no priority aging (a fast tier would starve background scans).

**Task-detail UI:** *no* UI has a live per-task drilldown. The wordflow `QueuePanel` modal fetches once and never refreshes; chrome's history never even shows priority; and the richest data — laravel's append-only `GlobalTaskEvent` transition log — is **indexed by task_id but surfaced nowhere**. The data backbone for a good detail view already exists; it's just unjoined.

The synthesis produced a concrete 10-item design (FAST priority tier + single bump endpoint, urgent-biased long-poll wake, both-clients dispatch, priority heaps in pycore + chrome, a `/api/task/{id}/detail` aggregate joining the event timeline, live drilldown UIs, priority aging). Most of it I can default sensibly. But four decisions genuinely fork the design and are yours to make — I don't want to build the central mechanism the wrong way.

## What I found and did

**A live bug was the real backbone.** Your task system's `TaskManagerService::submitResult()` called two methods that don't exist (`validateResultShape`, `failTaskInTransaction`) and read a return value from a `: void` method — so **every non-demo `completed` worker result fataled at runtime**. That's almost certainly a big part of the "doesn't fully work" feeling. I implemented the two missing methods (mirroring the existing failure/retry semantics + an audit `GlobalTaskEvent`) and changed the wrapper to `: ?int`. Verified at runtime: service resolves, return types correct, and 13/13 result-shape cases pass. `php -l` clean. The fix is additive and reversible.

**The root architectural problem:** there are **two parallel task substrates** — the generic `GlobalTask` system (used for images) and the AppQyV1 dictionary-column queues (used for translation/word-audio; sentence audio is synchronous inline). One "jump to top" can't currently prioritize a word across all three media types because they live in different places. Your decision to **unify them** is the right call — and the design reconciliation produced a strictly reversible 7-phase migration (add → dual-write → backfill → cutover → defer drops), nailed the cross-system contracts (the `remote_fast` capability-typed lane, the `interactive` flag, the SSE detail stream), and even **rejected hallucinated files** the design agents invented and corrected the transport from Reverb (dead here) to **SSE**.

## The constraint you need to weigh

This sandbox **cannot run your stack**: no `pdo_pgsql` driver (so no migrations / DB), and no Octane / pycore service / chrome extension. I can *write and lint* all remaining code, and run pure-logic reflection tests (as I did for Phase 1), but I **cannot prove phases 2–7 work by running them here** — that has to happen in your environment. The plan is explicitly per-phase verifiable, so the honest path is: I implement, you (or I, if you run commands via the `!` prefix) verify in the real env before each risky step.

Phases 2–4 are laravel backend, additive and reversible. Phases 5–7 (dual-write, backfill, cutover, retiring the legacy timers) are the hard-to-reverse, data-touching ones.

1	<?php

5	use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Schema;
6	use Illuminate\Database\Schema\Blueprint;
7	use Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB;
8	
9	/**
10	 * Safe Migration Helper
11	 *
12	 * Provides idempotent database migration tools that guarantee:
13	 * - Tables and data are never dropped
14	 * - Tables are created only when they do not exist
15	 * - Columns are added only when they do not exist
16	 * - Column type expansion is supported (e.g. string(50) -> string(255)), but never shrinking
17	 * - Index alignment is supported (adds missing indexes)
18	 * - Code is aligned to the database structure (not by rebuilding the table)
19	 *
20	 * Use cases:
21	 * - Table missing: create the table and all columns
22	 * - Table exists but a column is missing: add the missing column
23	 * - Table exists but a column type needs widening: expand the column type (no shrinking)
24	 * - Table exists but an index is missing: add the missing index
25	 */
26	class SafeMigrationHelper
27	{
28	    /**
29	     * Safely create a table (if it does not exist)
30	     *
31	     * @param string $connection Connection name
32	     * @param string $tableName Table name
33	     * @param callable $tableDefinition Table definition closure
34	     * @return array ['status' => 'created'|'exists', 'message' => string]
35	     */
36	    public static function safeCreateTable(
37	        string $connection,
38	        string $tableName,
39	        callable $tableDefinition
40	    ): array {
41	        $schema = Schema::connection($connection);
42	        
43	        if (!$schema->hasTable($tableName)) {
44	            $schema->create($tableName, $tableDefinition);
45	            return [
46	                'status' => 'created',
47	                'message' => "Table {$tableName} created successfully"
48	            ];
49	        }
50	        
51	        return [
52	            'status' => 'exists',
53	            'message' => "Table {$tableName} already exists"
54	        ];

12	 * OctaneTimerServiceProvider - Laravel Schedule Single Heartbeat Center
13	 *
14	 * This provider registers a single Octane tick that runs Laravel Schedule every second.
15	 * This allows sub-minute tasks (everySecond, everyFiveSeconds, etc.) to work in Octane.
16	 *
17	 * To add a new timer task:
18	 * 1. Create a class in app/Services/TimerTasks/
19	 * 2. Implement OctaneTimerTaskInterface (or extend OctaneTimerTaskAbstract)
20	 * 3. Define getName(), getInterval(), and exec() methods
21	 * 4. Task will be auto-discovered in routes/console.php and registered to Laravel Schedule
22	 *
23	 * This follows the Common Timer Design Specification:
24	 * - Single heartbeat center (1-second Octane tick)
25	 * - Standard Laravel Schedule (official Laravel pattern)
26	 * - Auto-discovery pattern (extensible via TimerTasks directory)
27	 * - Resource efficient (one timer loop drives all tasks)
28	 * - Fully compatible with Laravel ecosystem
29	 */

<?php

namespace App\Services\TimerTasks;

use App\Apps\AppQyV1\AppQyV1Services\AppQyV1UnifiedTTSQueueService;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Log;

/**
 * TTS Generation Task — queue-less operation.
 *
 * Octane timer task that drives TTS generation directly against the
 * canonical tables (no intermediate tts_queue table anymore):
 *   - words:    pending tts_cache_{lang} rows are claimed via
 *               AppQyV1DictionaryTTSCoordinator and generated inline;
 *   - articles: pending {lang}_article_library rows are split into
 *               sentences, generated, and written back to the article row;
 *   - sentences: stateless — generated synchronously at request time by
 *               AppQyV1UnifiedTTSQueueService::addTask, never by this timer.
 *
 * Stale processing claims are reaped at the start of every run, so crashed
 * processors (this timer or external pycore workers) never strand rows.
 */
class AppQyV1TTSGenerationTask extends OctaneTimerTaskAbstract
{
    private $queueService;
    // Use intelligent batch size (dynamically adjusted based on performance)
    // null = auto-detect optimal batch size based on success rate
    private $queueBatchSize = null;

    public function __construct()
    {
        $this->queueService = new AppQyV1UnifiedTTSQueueService();
    }

    public function getName(): string
    {
        return 'appqyv1_tts_generation';
    }

    public function getInterval(): int
    {
        return 60;
    }

    public function exec(): void

All three clients are implemented and type-checked clean (pycore py_compile, chrome vue-tsc, FE tsc — zero new errors each). But the agents honestly flagged two real **integration seams** that would stop the fast path from actually working end-to-end:

1. **Backend gap**: the FE sends `interactive:true` to the *AppQyV1 translation/TTS queue* endpoints, but those controllers don't forward `interactive` to `createTask` — so no `remote_fast` task is created. I need to wire this on the backend.

## Summary

Both correctness bugs in the chrome-mcp task-center fast-lane integration are fixed. All work is under `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/apps/mcp-chrome/app/chrome-extension/`. Note the real path has an extra `app/chrome-extension/` segment vs. what the task stated.

### Files changed

1. **`entrypoints/background/services/task-center/SimpleWorkerBase.ts`** — Made the fast lane per-worker and opt-in:
   - Removed the global default `capabilities: config.capabilities ?? CHROME_FAST_CAPABILITIES`. It now defaults to `[]`, so a subclass that passes nothing advertises nothing.
   - `withFastLane(types, capabilities)` now adds `'remote_fast'` **only when `capabilities.length &gt; 0`** — a worker with no real capability never registers for the fast lane.
   - Added `normalizeCapabilities()` to validate/de-dup advertised tags against the exact vocabulary (`audio|image|translate|sentence_audio`), so a typo can't widen what a worker claims.
   - Updated doc comments; `CHROME_FAST_CAPABILITIES` is now documented as the Bing worker's set, not a global default.

2. **`entrypoints/background/services/task-center/processors/GeminiImageProcessor.ts`** — Set `capabilities: ['image']`. The Gemini worker only handles `gemini_image` → image generation, so it serves just `image` and (via the base) opts into `remote_fast` for image tasks only.

3. **`entrypoints/background/services/task-center/processors/NotebookLMProcessor.ts`** — Set `capabilities: []`. NotebookLM is a Q&amp;A worker whose `notebooklm` task type maps to **none** of the four fast-lane capabilities, so it stays off the fast lane entirely (no `remote_fast`, no capabilities).

4. **`entrypoints/background/services/bing-dictionary-worker-service.ts`** (Bug 2, the real worker) — In `registerBackend()`:
   - Added `'remote_fast'` to `processor_types` (now `['remote_translation','remote_client','remote_fast']`).
   - Added `capabilities: CHROME_FAST_CAPABILITIES` (`['image','translate','sentence_audio']`), imported single-sourced from `SimpleWorkerBase`. Bing scrapes a translation + sample-image bytes + pronunciation audio for every word, so it genuinely executes all three. It does **not** advertise `audio` (pycore owns API-driven sentence TTS).

### Per-worker capability rationale
- **Bing dictionary worker** → `['image','translate','sentence_audio']` — produces translation, sample images, and pronunciation audio per word (handles `word_translation` + `word_media`).
- **Gemini image worker** → `['image']` — only generates images (`gemini_image`).
- **NotebookLM worker** → `[]` (off the fast lane) — Q&amp;A, no matching fast capability.
- DeepSeek and GoogleNews processors are placeholders with no worker registration (verified: no `register`/`start`/`processor_types`), so nothing to change.

### Typecheck
Ran `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` in `app/chrome-extension`. **Zero errors in any file I touched** (and zero in the entire `services/task-center`, `bing-dictionary`, `gemini-image`, `notebooklm`, and `api/WorkerApiClient` paths). The 394 total errors are all pre-existing/unrelated — missing `vitest` module and `@/entrypoints/...` resolution in `tests/`, plus `wxt.config.ts`.

### Remaining concerns
- I did not touch laravel_main or the contract, as instructed. The capability mapping assumes `sentence_audio` (not `audio`) is the correct tag for Bing's word-pronunciation mp3 — this matches the comments already in `WorkerApiClient.ts` ("chrome-mcp serves `['image','translate','sentence_audio']`; pycore serves `['audio','translate']`"). If the server's enqueue side tags single-word pronunciation requests as `audio` rather than `sentence_audio`, the Bing worker would not pick them up — worth confirming against the laravel enqueue tagging, but that's outside this repo's scope.
- Cannot run the extension (no browser), so verification is static only.</result>

[2026-06-22 17:04:22] ASSISTANT:
All seven phases are implemented and statically verified. Here's the complete picture.

## What I found

Two things mattered more than the original request:

1. **A live runtime bug was the real blocker.** `TaskManagerService::submitResult()` called two methods that didn't exist (`validateResultShape`, `failTaskInTransaction`) and read a return from a `: void` method — so **every non-demo `completed` worker result fataled.** That's almost certainly the core of "doesn't fully work." Fixed and runtime-verified.

2. **The "two substrates" were even more split than the map showed.** Images already flow through `GlobalTask` (via `AppQyV1WordMediaService`); translation + word-audio used the dict-column queues; and **audio is actually generated by pycore over a separate "assist protocol," not the GlobalTask worker API.** This shaped every design decision and is why the substrate-unification pieces ship **flag-gated off**.

## What's built (all 4 subsystems, ~30 files)

A unified, capability-typed **`remote_fast` lane** that both clients share (first-idle-wins, runs once), driven by an explicit FE **`interactive`** flag, with a **live SSE task-detail drilldown**:

- **laravel_main** (17 files, all `php -l` + tinker-reflection clean): the `submitResult` fix; `remote_fast` lane + `capability`/`is_fast_tier` schema; capability-filtered claim; `pending_fast` in pull/heartbeat; `POST /api/task/{id}/bump`; `GET /api/task/{id}/detail` + `…/stream` (SSE tailing `global_task_events`, no Reverb); priority **aging** (anti-starvation); flag-gated substrate dual-write + `syncToDictRow`; an idempotent **backfill** command; and `interactive` wired end-to-end through the translation queue.
- **pycore** (`py_compile` clean): registers `remote_fast` + caps `[audio,translate]`, a priority heap on the translation worker, jittered fast-poll on `pending_fast`, and `fast_lane` status surfaced to the UI.
- **chrome-mcp** (`vue-tsc` clean): per-worker capabilities (Bing→`[image,translate,sentence_audio]`, Gemini→`[image]`, NotebookLM→off-lane), priority-sorted claims, fast re-poll, and a new `TaskDetailModal.vue` EventSource SSE drilldown.
- **wordflow FE** (`tsc` clean): `interactive:true` on user lookups, a live SSE drilldown in `QueuePanel` with status timeline + "Bump to top," and an interactive/priority control in `AssistRequestModal`.

The fast path now: **user lookup → `interactive=true` → `remote_fast` task @priority 100, capability `translate` → idle eligible client claims sub-second → live SSE modal streams pending→completed.**

## Honest status on verification

This sandbox has **no `pdo_pgsql` driver** and can't run Octane/pycore/the browser, so I verified everything by `php -l`, `artisan tinker` reflection (Phase 1 logic: 13/13 cases; full chain boots), `py_compile`, and `vue-tsc`/`tsc --noEmit` — **all clean**. It is **not yet proven running against your stack.** The runbook is in **`development-guides/UNIFIED_TASK_SYSTEM_AS_BUILT.md`** (and the full design in `…_UPGRADE_PLAN.md`).

To bring it up in your env: `php artisan migrate --force` (additive/idempotent), register the clients, do an interactive lookup, watch the SSE modal. Everything risky is **off by default** (`APPQYV1_DUAL_WRITE_GLOBAL`, `APPQYV1_DUAL_WRITE_IMAGE`), and rollback is "drop `remote_fast` from registration + flags off → legacy path still serves."

## Two follow-ups you should know about (documented in the as-built doc)

- **Audio interactive path:** translation interactive is fully wired; the **audio** interactive path needs the same `interactive` threading in `AppQyV1TTSQueueController::batchAddTasks` (or route audio via `createInteractiveTask`) — I left it rather than mis-wire an endpoint I couldn't trace.
- **Audio generation route:** a `remote_fast`/`remote_audio` audio task has no writeback processor (audio uses the assist protocol), so `syncToDictRow` only projects *status* — validate the actual audio-file path before enabling `APPQYV1_DUAL_WRITE_GLOBAL`.

I saved the non-obvious architecture (two substrates, audio-via-assist-protocol, SSE-not-Reverb) to memory for future sessions. Want me to wire the audio interactive path through `AppQyV1TTSQueueController` next, or set up the env-side verification steps?

[2026-06-22 17:10:25]   <- tool result:
=== service batchAddTasks signature + position usage ===
954:    public function batchAddTasks(array $tasks, string $position = 'end'): array
    public function batchAddTasks(array $tasks, string $position = 'end'): array
    {
        $results = [];

        foreach ($tasks as $index => $taskData) {
            if (!isset($taskData['content']) || !isset($taskData['language'])) {
                $results[] = [
                    'success' => false,
                    'error' => 'Missing required fields (content, language)',
                    'index' => $index,
                ];
                continue;
            }

            $content = $taskData['content'];
            $language = $taskData['language'];
            $type = $taskData['type'] ?? null;
            $taskPosition = $taskData['position'] ?? $position;

            $result = $this->addTask($content, $language, $type, $taskPosition);
            $result['index'] = $index;
            $result['content'] = $content;

            $results[] = $result;
        }

        return [
            'success' => true,
            'total' => count($tasks),
            'results' => $results,
        ];

## What I just finished

**Audio interactive path — wired** (it was open issue #1). When the wordflow FE sends `interactive:true` to the TTS queue endpoints (`addTask` / `batchAddTasks`), the request now forces `position='beginning'` → `tts_priority=100`.

The key decision here was respecting the **real architecture** rather than blindly mirroring the translation path: audio is generated by pycore over the **assist protocol** (which claims highest-`tts_priority` dict rows first) and has **no GlobalTask writeback processor** — so routing interactive audio onto a `remote_fast` GlobalTask would create a task that never produces audio. Front-of-queue is the actually-working fast path, and that priority still propagates into the linked GlobalTask when `APPQYV1_DUAL_WRITE_GLOBAL` is enabled. Added `interactive` to both TTS FormRequests for cleanliness.

Verified: `php -l` clean on all three files, controller resolves, both FormRequests carry the `interactive` rule. As-built doc updated.

## Where the whole task stands now

All 7 phases plus this follow-up are implemented and statically verified (`php -l`, tinker reflection, `py_compile`, `vue-tsc`/`tsc`). Both fast paths are now end-to-end on the backend: **translation interactive** via the `remote_fast` lane, **audio interactive** via front-of-queue + the assist protocol.

What genuinely **remains** can only be done in your environment (no `pdo_pgsql`/Octane/pycore/browser here):

- **Run the verification runbook** in `development-guides/UNIFIED_TASK_SYSTEM_AS_BUILT.md` — migrate, register clients, do an interactive lookup, watch the SSE detail modal stream live, confirm pycore prioritises the bumped audio row.
- **Validate the GlobalTask audio-generation route** before enabling `APPQYV1_DUAL_WRITE_GLOBAL` (no writeback processor exists for `remote_audio` completions — `syncToDictRow` only projects status).
